<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}

<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the area of this triangle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Height of triangle}     &= 6 - 2 \\\\
                              &= 4 \\\\
\text{Base of triangle}       &= 6 - 4 \\\\
                              &= 2 \\\\
\text{Area of triangle}       &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text{Height of triangle ABC} \times \text{Base of triangle ABC} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 4 \times 2 \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {4 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                              &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>

                              &= 2 \\\\
\text{Area of triangle}       &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text{Height of triangle ABC} \times \text{Base of triangle ABC} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 4 \times 2 \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {4 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                              &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

![st-georges-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-19-00.png](/assets/st-georges-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-19-00.png "st-georges-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-19-00.png")

The pie chart shows the proportions of different colours of cars in a school carpark. there were $36$ black cars. How many blue cars were there?   

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{black}                             &:& \text{blue} \\\\
= & 40 \%                                    &:& 30 \% \\\\
= & 1                                        &:& \dfrac {30} {40} \\\\
= & 1 \times 36                              &:& \dfrac {30 \times 36} {40} \\\\
= & 36                                       &:& \dfrac {3 \times 10 \times 4 \times 9} {4 \times 10} \\\\
= & 36                                       &:& \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel 4 \times 9} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
= & 36                                       &:& 3 \times 9 \\\\
= & 36                                       &:& 27
\end{array}
$

Number of blue cars $= 36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{black}                             &:& \text{blue} \\\\
= & 40 \%                                    &:& 30 \% \\\\
= & 1                                        &:& \dfrac {30} {40} \\\\
= & 1 \times 36                              &:& \dfrac {30 \times 36} {40} \\\\
= & 36                                       &:& \dfrac {3 \times 10 \times 4 \times 9} {4 \times 10} \\\\
= & 36                                       &:& \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel 4 \times 9} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
= & 36                                       &:& 3 \times 9 \\\\
= & 36                                       &:& 27
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of blue cars $= 36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

- $14$
- $30$
- $16$
- $21$

\begin{aligned}

                                                            &= 15
\end{aligned}

\end{aligned}

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

The chart below shows the mileage between five places in North London. For example it gives the distance between Kenton and 
Hampstead as $4 \ \text{miles}$.

![the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2011--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-16-00.png](/assets/the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2011--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-16-00.png "the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2011--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-16-00.png")

Daisy lives in Hampstead and drives her son to school at Haberdashers' in the morning before continuing on to the office in Southgate. 
She then visits her sister in Harpenden before driving home via Habs. How far does she travel altogether?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

For such questions, it will be easier to write down the route taken.


$
\begin{matrix}
\text {Hampstead}  &\rightarrow&     \text {Habs},  &\rightarrow&     \text{ Southgate},   &\rightarrow&     \text{ Harpenden}  &\rightarrow&   \text {via Habs}  &\rightarrow&     \text {Hampsted} \\
(home)  &&      (school)  &&      (office)  &&      (sister)  &&     &&     (home) \\\\
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{(10)}} 
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+9)}}  
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+18)}}   
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+13)}}  
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+10)}} \\
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
      & = 10 + 9 + 18 + 13 + 10         \\\\
      & = 60                             \\\\
      & = 60 \ \text{miles}                               
\end{aligned}
$

She travelled altogether $= 60 \ \text{miles}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

 $= 60 \ \text{miles}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>For such questions, it will be easier to write down the route taken.
<emptyline>

$
\begin{matrix}
\text {Hampstead}  &\rightarrow&     \text {Habs},  &\rightarrow&     \text{ Southgate},   &\rightarrow&     \text{ Harpenden}  &\rightarrow&   \text {via Habs}  &\rightarrow&     \text {Hampsted} \\
(home)  &&      (school)  &&      (office)  &&      (sister)  &&     &&     (home) \\\\
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{(10)}} 
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+9)}}  
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+18)}}   
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+13)}}  
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+10)}} \\
\end{matrix}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
      & = 10 + 9 + 18 + 13 + 10         \\\\
      & = 60                             \\\\
      & = 60 \ \text{miles}                               
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
She travelled altogether $= 60 \ \text{miles}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'> $= 60 \ \text{miles}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Every evening Diana has a bath. She turns on the taps and waits patiently for the bath to fill before she turns them off and steps in. At the end of her bath she steps out of the water before pulling out the plug to empty the bath. 

A graph of the volume of water (measured in litres) plotted against time (measured in minutes) is shown below.

</div>

<div class='working'>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Amount of time she spend in the bath}     &= 25 - 5 \\\\
                                                &= 20 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                                &= 20 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the number of litres of water that flow into the bath **per minute** as the bath fills up.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Amount of water flowing per minute}   &= \dfrac {1000} {5} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 200} {5} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 200} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                            &= 200 \ \text{litres}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$200 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}

                                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 200} {5} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 200} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                            &= 200 \ \text{litres}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>

Does the bath empty at a faster, slower or the same rate as it fills?

<div class='question question'>

The diagram below shows a sequence of squares drawn on a grid.

The y-coordinate of the centre of one of the squares in this sequence is $2177$. Work out the x-coordinate of this square.

![the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2011--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-28-subquestion-5-00.png](/assets/the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2011--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-28-subquestion-5-00.png "the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2011--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-28-subquestion-5-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the x-coordinate $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
2 \times a - 1      &= 2177 \\\\
2a - 1              &= 2177 \\\\
2a                  &= 2177 + 1 \\\\
2a                  &= 2178 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {2178} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 1089} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 1089} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                   &= 1089
\end {aligned}
$

X-coordinate of the center of the square $= 1089$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1089$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the x-coordinate $= a$
<emptyline>

a                   &= 1089
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
X-coordinate of the center of the square $= 1089$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A group of people chose their preferred Olympic sport from a list of five: Athletics, Swimming, Cycling, Hockey and Gymnastics.

The proportions choosing each sport are shown on the pie chart:

![the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-18-00.png](/assets/the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-18-00.png "the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-18-00.png")

</div>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What percentage of people chose Athletics?


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Percentage of people who chose Athletics}     &= 100 - 24 - 8 - 20 - 12 \\\\
                                                    &= 36 \ \%
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36 \ \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}

                                                    &= 36 \ \%
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $45$ people chose Athletics, how many chose Hockey?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{athletics}                        &:& \text{hockey} \\\\
= & 36                                      &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {12} {36} \\\\
= & 1 \times 45                             &:& \dfrac {12 \times 45} {36} \\\\
= & 45                                      &:& \dfrac {12 \times 15 \times 3} {12 \times 3} \\\\
= & 45                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 15 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 3} \\\\
= & 45                                      &:& 15
\end{array}
$

Number of people who choose $= 15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{athletics}                        &:& \text{hockey} \\\\
= & 36                                      &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {12} {36} \\\\
= & 1 \times 45                             &:& \dfrac {12 \times 45} {36} \\\\
= & 45                                      &:& \dfrac {12 \times 15 \times 3} {12 \times 3} \\\\
= & 45                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 15 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 3} \\\\
= & 45                                      &:& 15
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of people who choose $= 15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

|    $2005$   |    $2006$   |     $2007$     |    $2008$   |    $2009$   |    $2010$   |    $2011$   |     $2012$     |     $2013$     |    $2014$   |    $2015$   |
|:-----------:|:-----------:|:--------------:|:-----------:|:-----------:|:-----------:|:-----------:|:--------------:|:--------------:|:-----------:|:-----------:|
| $\pounds 3$ | $\pounds 4$ | $\pounds 4.50$ | $\pounds 5$ | $\pounds 6$ | $\pounds 4$ | $\pounds 1$ | $\pounds 1.50$ | $\pounds 1.50$ | $\pounds 4$ | $\pounds 4$ |

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Vivek buys $5000$ shares in $2005$ and sells these shares in $2015$.  Work out the total profit made.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Profit}   &= \text{Selling price} - \text{Cost price} \\\\
                &= 5000 \times 5 - 5000 \times 3 \\\\
                &= 25000 - 15000 \\\\
                &= \pounds 10000
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 10000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Profit}   &= \text{Selling price} - \text{Cost price} \\\\
                &= 5000 \times 5 - 5000 \times 3 \\\\
                &= 25000 - 15000 \\\\
                &= \pounds 10000
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 10000$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Peter buys $1500$ shares in $2009$ and sells them in a later year. Given that he makes a loss of $\pounds 3000$, 
find the two possible years in which he might have sold his shares.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Loss per share}       &= \dfrac {3000} {1500} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {1500 \times 2} {1500} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {1500} \times 2} {\cancel {1500}} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 2 \\\\
\text{Selling price of share} &= 6 - 2\\\\
                              &= \pounds 4
\end{aligned}
$

Two possible years $= 2010, 2014$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2010, 2014$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Loss per share}       &= \dfrac {3000} {1500} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {1500 \times 2} {1500} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {1500} \times 2} {\cancel {1500}} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 2 \\\\
\text{Selling price of share} &= 6 - 2\\\\
                              &= \pounds 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Two possible years $= 2010, 2014$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2010, 2014$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

A group of $48$ children are asked to choose their favourite subject from a list of three: English, Mathematics and Science.

The results are displayed on a pie chart:

![the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-20-00.png](/assets/the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-20-00.png "the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-20-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of the group chose English?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of group choosing English}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {90} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {90}} {\cancel {90} \times 4} \\\\ 
                                              &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of group choosing English}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {90} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {90}} {\cancel {90} \times 4} \\\\ 
                                              &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {4}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $33 \dfrac{1}{3}\%$ chose Mathematics work out the angle of the Mathematics sector.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Angle of Mathematics sector}  &= 33 \dfrac {1} {3} \% \ \text{of} \ 360 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {33 \dfrac {1} {3}} {100} \times 360 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\dfrac {100} {3}} {100} \times 360 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {100} {3 \times 100} \times 360 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {100 \times 360} {3 \times 100} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {100 \times 3 \times 120} {3 \times 100} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {100} \times \cancel 3 \times 120} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
                                    &= 120 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \degree$

</div>

                                    &= \dfrac {33 \dfrac {1} {3}} {100} \times 360 \\\\

                                    &= \dfrac {100 \times 3 \times 120} {3 \times 100} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {100} \times \cancel 3 \times 120} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
                                    &= 120 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

How many children chose Science?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
  \text{Number of children choosing Science}    &= \dfrac {360 - 90 - 120} {360} \times 48 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {150} {360} \times 48 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {150 \times 48} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {30 \times 5 \times 12 \times 4} {30 \times 12} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times 5 \times \cancel {12} \times 4} {\cancel {30} \times \cancel {12}} \\\\
                                                &= 5 \times 4 \\\\
                                                &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the area of shape $A$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Height of the triangle}   &= 2 - 1 \\\\
                                &= 1 \\\\
\text{Base of the triangle}     &= 5 - 2 \\\\
                                &= 3 \\\\
\text{Area of the triangle}     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text{Height of the triangle} \times \text{Base of the triangle} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 1 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
\text{Area of the triangle}     &= 2 \times \text{Area of the triangle} \\\\
                                &= 2 \times \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Height of the triangle}   &= 2 - 1 \\\\
                                &= 1 \\\\
\text{Base of the triangle}     &= 5 - 2 \\\\
                                &= 3 \\\\
\text{Area of the triangle}     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text{Height of the triangle} \times \text{Base of the triangle} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 1 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
\text{Area of the triangle}     &= 2 \times \text{Area of the triangle} \\\\
                                &= 2 \times \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

Mark  and Sarah each has a bag of coloured counters.

These pie charts show the proportion of each colour in their bags.

![the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-10-00.png](/assets/the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-10-00.png "the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-10-00.png")
![the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-10-01.png](/assets/the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-10-01.png "the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-10-01.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>

</div>
</div>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</div>

</code></pre>

</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What percentage of Mark's counters are blue?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of blue in Mark's counter}   &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 100 \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {90 \times 100} {360} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {90 \times 25 \times 4} {4 \times 90} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times 25 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4} \times \cancel {90}} \\\\
                                              &= 25 \ \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25 \ \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of blue in Mark's counter}   &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 100 \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {90 \times 100} {360} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {90 \times 25 \times 4} {4 \times 90} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times 25 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4} \times \cancel {90}} \\\\
                                              &= 25 \ \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25 \ \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Mark has 40 counters in his bag.

How many of his counters are not blue?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using value from the answer above,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of counters that are not blue}   &= (100 - 25) \% \ \text{of} \ 40 \\\\
                                              &= 75 \% \ \text{of} \ 40 \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {75} {100} \times 40 \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {75 \times 40} {100} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {25 \times 3 \times 4 \times 10} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 10} {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                              &= 3 \times 10 \\\\
                                              &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using value from the answer above,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

                                              &= 75 \% \ \text{of} \ 40 \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {75} {100} \times 40 \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {75 \times 40} {100} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {25 \times 3 \times 4 \times 10} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 10} {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                              &= 3 \times 10 \\\\
                                              &= 30
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$30$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\dfrac{1}{3}$ of Sarah's counters are red, and the rest are green or blue.

Given that Sarah has $12$ red counters, how many counters are there altogether in her bag?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of counters Sarah has $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {3} \times a       &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {3}                &= 12 \\\\
a                             &= 12 \times 3 \\\\
a                             &= 36
\end{aligned}
$

Total number of counters Sarah has $= 36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of counters Sarah has $= a$
<emptyline>

$

\dfrac {a} {3}                &= 12 \\\\
a                             &= 12 \times 3 \\\\
a                             &= 36
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total number of counters Sarah has $= 36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Sarah has twice as many green counters as blue ones. 

How many blue counters does she have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of blue counters Sarah has $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a + 12         &= 36 \\\\
3a + 12             &= 36 \\\\
3a                  &= 36 - 12 \\\\
3a                  &= 24 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {24} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 8} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 8} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                   &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

Number of blue counters Sarah has $= 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of blue counters Sarah has $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a + 12         &= 36 \\\\
3a + 12             &= 36 \\\\
3a                  &= 36 - 12 \\\\
3a                  &= 24 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {24} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 8} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 8} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                   &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of blue counters Sarah has $= 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

They each pick one counter at random from their own bag.

Who is more likely to pick a green counter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Probability of picking green counter from Mark's bag}     &= \dfrac {180} {360} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {180} {180 \times 2} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {180}} {\cancel {180} \times 2} \\\\
                                                                &= 0.5 \\\\
\text{Probability of picking green counter from Sarah's bag}    &= \dfrac {8} {36} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 2} {4 \times 9} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 2} {\cancel 4 \times 9} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {2} {9} \\\\
                                                                &= 0.22
\end{aligned}
$

Mark is more likely to pick a green counter.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Mark

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Probability of picking green counter from Mark's bag}     &= \dfrac {180} {360} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {180} {180 \times 2} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {180}} {\cancel {180} \times 2} \\\\
                                                                &= 0.5 \\\\
\text{Probability of picking green counter from Sarah's bag}    &= \dfrac {8} {36} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 2} {4 \times 9} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 2} {\cancel 4 \times 9} \\\\
                                                                &= \dfrac {2} {9} \\\\
                                                                &= 0.22
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Mark is more likely to pick a green counter.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

How many oranges are needed to make $25 \ \text{litres}$ of juice?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Oranges needed} &= 13 \times 25 \\\\
                       &= 325 \ \text{oranges}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{juice}                             &:& \text{oranges} \\\\
= & 1                                        &:& 13 \\\\
= & 1 \times 25                              &:& 13 \times 25 \\\\
= & 25                                       &:& 325
\end{array}
$

Number of oranges needed $= 325$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$325$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Oranges needed} &= 13 \times 25 \\\\
                       &= 325 \ \text{oranges}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

= & 25                                       &:& 325
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of oranges needed $= 325$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Sarah measured the temperature of the beaker again $4 \ \text{hours}$ and $30 \ \text{minutes}$ after the last reading on the graph.

At what time did she take this measurement?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time when she took the reading}   &= 10 {:} 50 \ \text{am} + 4 \ \text{hours} \ 30 \ \text{min} \\\\
                                        &= 10 {:} 50 \ \text{am} + 4 \times 60 \ \text{min} + 30 \ \text{min} \\\\
                                        &= 10 {:} 50 \ \text{am} + 4 \times 60 + 30 \\\\
                                        &= 10 {:} 50 + 240 + 30 \\\\
                                        &= 10 {:} 50 + 270 \\\\
                                        &= 3 {:} 20 \ \text{pm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 {:} 20 \ \text{pm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note

                                        &= 10 {:} 50 \ \text{am} + 4 \times 60 \ \text{min} + 30 \ \text{min} \\\\
                                        &= 10 {:} 50 \ \text{am} + 4 \times 60 + 30 \\\\
                                        &= 10 {:} 50 + 240 + 30 \\\\
                                        &= 10 {:} 50 + 270 \\\\
                                        &= 3 {:} 20 \ \text{pm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3 {:} 20 \ \text{pm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
